# Effect review — every candidate effect, three variants each

**This is not a feature design.** It is a content-selection round. Every question is one
candidate effect, and every answer is a ruling on whether that effect enters the game and in
which form. Nothing here specifies how anything is built.

**1,177 questions.** There is no branching: every question is independent, so the count you see is
the count you answer. Rejecting is one keystroke.

## 0. How to review this document

**Every question is one effect.** The three lettered options are three versions of the *same*
effect — sometimes three strengths, sometimes three mechanisms, sometimes three scopes,
whichever fork was worth putting to you for that particular idea. Pick the one you want.

- **(a) (b) (c)** — the three versions. Pick one and that version enters the CSV as approved.
- **(d) reject** — the effect does not enter the game in any form.
- **Write your own** — the free-text box is on every question, always. If none of the three is
  right, type the version you want; your words go into the CSV verbatim as the approved effect,
  not a paraphrase of them.

**One option is always marked as the recommendation, and it is never (d).** Enter presses whatever
is marked, so holding Enter approves rather than rejects. The mark moves to *use what I wrote* the
moment you type, so Enter can never discard what you wrote. Accepting the recommendation without
moving the mark is recorded as its own state — being shown what Enter does is not the same as
choosing it.

**Answers are revisitable.** Go back to any earlier question and change it; nothing is lost.

### What each question header tells you

```
**Name** — slot, class code, provenance. One line saying what the effect is.
```

- **slot** — where the effect lives on a card: `suit`, `rank`, `type`, `stamp`, `skill`,
  `consumable`, `rule`, or `status`. Every effect here fits one of them; that was the filter for
  getting in at all.
- **class code** — its cell in the design-space taxonomy (`C4`, `H6`, `P3`…). Two effects sharing
  a class code are competing for the same design space, which is why they are next to each other.
- **provenance** — the document or wiki it was mined from, or `generated` if it was written to
  fill a class the corpus left empty.

### The order

Grouped by family, then by class within the family. **An upgrade sits directly after the effect
it upgrades** — 11 questions are positioned that way, so when you see a stronger or less
restricted version of the question you just answered, you are being asked to price the pair.

---

## 1. What was mined, and what happened to it

**2,234 candidate effects** were extracted from every text document in the repo and from the
Balatro and Cryptid reference wikis:

| Source | Extracted |
|---|---|
| `CARD_CATALOG.csv` | 378 |
| `DESIGN_DOC.md`, `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md`, `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` | 758 |
| the braindump, the random-effects sheet, the curated pre-grid sheet, `todo.md`, `pokerpatience.txt` | 459 |
| balatrowiki.org — jokers, modifiers, stakes, blinds, tags, challenges | 249 |
| Cryptid and Pokermon mod wikis | 390 |

148 were folded as exact restatements, leaving 2,086 to judge. **1,160 were dropped:**

| Reason | Dropped |
|---|---|
| `DUPLICATE` — the same design space as an effect already asked about | 747 |
| `NO_MECHANIC` — art direction, naming, engineering, or a taxonomy heading | 130 |
| `RESKIN` — a numeric or suit reskin of a more general effect | 119 |
| `ALREADY_A_RULE` — restates a rule the game already has | 113 |
| `NOT_APPLICABLE` — needs a held hand, discards per round, or blinds | 51 |

**926 survived and were rewritten as grid-native mechanics** — foreign vocabulary translated, and
reach expressed in this game's four-dimensional coordinate wherever the effect could carry it.
**131 more were written from scratch**: 69 to fill taxonomy classes the corpus left empty, 62
for eighteen classes the taxonomy itself had failed to name — progress-relative scoring, placement
order, deck-composition reads, slot topology, in-run quests, and the whole of family X below.

**One hundred and eight more were mined from external games** whose designs cover ground Balatro and
Cryptid structurally cannot: Santorini (a 5×5 grid you build height on, whose god powers are
explicit rule-breakers), the Zachtronics Solitaire Collection and Hempuli's *A Solitaire Mystery*
(both cited in your own braindump), Concrete Jungle, Luck be a Landlord, Backpack Battles,
Ballionaire and the tile-placement board games; then Open-Face Chinese Poker and the poker variant
family, Teamfight Tactics, Super Auto Pets, the solitaire-variant literature, mahjong hand
catalogues, incremental games, Blue Prince, Inscryption, Loop Hero, Monster Train, Baba Is You,
Photosynthesis, 2048 and the match-3 line. Neither reference wiki has a board, so the grid, height,
class-synergy and prop families got almost nothing from them; these do. **Every source considered,
mined or deliberately skipped, is registered in `build/SOURCES.md`.**

**Ten more were recovered by re-reading the braindump by hand.** The mining pass skipped that
file's art-direction and circus-history sections wholesale, and mechanics had been written
parenthetically inside them. Those ten carry their braindump line number in the question text.

**Every source was then line-audited.** `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` was checked row by row against its
own hook tags — 50 rows propose only a name or a visual and are correctly absent; of the 391 that
propose a mechanic, all but two were already present. the curated pre-grid sheet is complete at
25 of 25. The random-effects sheet, `DESIGN_DOC.md` and `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md` came back
clean. `todo.md` and `pokerpatience.txt` are engineering and layout backlog and were correctly
skipped wholesale.

Every drop is recorded with its reason and, for duplicates, the id of the effect it was folded
into — so any category can be resurfaced if you disagree with a call.

## 2. The design space this covers

The taxonomy behind the class codes is **24 families, 226 classes**, crossed with six structural
axes (slot, trigger, scope, duration, valence, agency). **216 of the 226 classes are represented
here.** The 10 that are not are exactly family N — view, camera and UI — **excluded by your
ruling**; the taxonomy still carries them so the hole stays visible.

The taxonomy was audited once the corpus pass was finished, and it had a bias worth naming: built
from the engine's hook surface and from Balatro, it described **effects that fire and add**, and
had almost no vocabulary for effects that *read state* or that key off *absence*. Eighteen classes
were added to correct it, including a new **family X — absence, failure and the unplayed**, which is
the mirror of every other family in the document.

---


## Family A - Scoring — the number itself


### A1 - Flat point add

- **Q0001** `[root]` — **Cheering Team card** — skill, A1, from `the random-effects sheet, line 5`. Pays its own suit · **(a)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points · **(b)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points, scaling with how many of them there are · **(c)** Each scored card sharing this card's suit gains extra points, and this card gains the same again · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0002** `[root]` — **Crowd glow** — rule, A1, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals`. The crowd trickles in regardless · **(a)** On a low-scoring turn, a small amount of fame arrives anyway · **(b)** On a turn that scores nothing at all, a small amount of fame arrives anyway · **(c)** On a low-scoring turn, fame arrives inversely to what you scored, so the worst turns pay most · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0003** `[root]` — **Extra Point** — skill, A1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Gain extra flat points on score (+10) · **(a)** +10 points when this card scores · **(b)** +2 points for each card in the line this card scores in · **(c)** +10 points when this card scores, doubling each further time it scores in the same show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0004** `[root]` — **The Pose (Sandow)** — skill, A1, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §B3 Strength & Endurance`. Scores by standing still · **(a)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, rather than by melding · **(b)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, paying more the longer it holds the pose · **(c)** Scores by being spotlit and doing nothing, and cannot score by melding at all · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0005** `[root]` — **The Sparkler** — skill, A1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Straight into the column score · **(a)** When this card scores, +10 flat points go to its column's score · **(b)** When this card scores, flat points equal to its rank go to its column's score · **(c)** When this card scores, +10 flat points go to whichever of its lines scored lowest · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### A2 - Additive mult

- **Q0006** `[root]` — **Bootstraps** — skill, A2, from `balatro`. Paid by what is in your pocket · **(a)** Gains a multiplier for every fixed amount of gold you hold · **(b)** Gains a multiplier for every fixed amount of gold, and spending gold mid-show lowers it immediately · **(c)** Gains a multiplier for gold held, capped, and converts overflow gold into flat points · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0007** `[root]` — **Combo card** — skill, A2, from `the random-effects sheet, line 6`. Scored cards keep the hand's value · **(a)** During scoring, each card gains temporary points equal to the hand it scored in · **(b)** During scoring, each card gains permanent points equal to the hand it scored in, once · **(c)** During scoring, each card gains temporary points equal to the hand it scored in, doubled if it scored in two lines · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0008** `[root]` — **Diamond type** — type, A2, from `the curated pre-grid sheet, line 92`. Twice the score, twice the price · **(a)** Doubles this card's score, and costs 3 more when traded at shops · **(b)** Doubles this card's score, and it cannot be sold at all · **(c)** Doubles this card's score, and every other card of its suit costs more at shops · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0009** `[root]` — **More Poker card** — skill, A2, from `the random-effects sheet, line 3`. Poker hands pay double · **(a)** Poker hands score double · **(b)** Poker hands score double, but only the highest hand of each act · **(c)** Poker hands score double, and non-poker melds score nothing · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0010** `[root]` — **Noble Family card** — skill, A2, from `the random-effects sheet, line 7`. Face cards feed each other · **(a)** Each scored face card raises the points of the next scored face card by 1 · **(b)** Each scored face card raises the next one by 1, and the chain resets at the end of each act · **(c)** Each scored face card raises the next by 1, with no reset, so the chain runs the whole show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0011** `[root]` — **Per-act score multiplier** — rule, A2, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §2 Core Game Loop refinements`. Later acts are worth more · **(a)** Each act carries a rising score multiplier, so the final act is worth the most · **(b)** Only the final act carries an extra multiplier · **(c)** Each act's multiplier rises, and skipping an act carries its multiplier into the next · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

Same suit, stacked hoops · **(a)** Consecutive same-suit cards in a line multiply their score together · **(b)** Consecutive same-suit cards in a line multiply their score, and the multiplier grows with run length · **(c)** Consecutive same-suit cards in a line multiply their score, counting across grid boundaries at the same row · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0013** `[root]` — **Joker Stencil** — skill, A3, from `balatro`. Paid by the room you left · **(a)** Gains a multiplier for every empty skill slot you are carrying · **(b)** Gains a multiplier for every empty cell on the grid at scoring · **(c)** Gains a multiplier for every empty skill slot, doubled if every slot but its own is empty · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0014** `[root]` — **Pagliacci** — skill, A3, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Gains bonus multiplier while this card carries any debuff or negative status · **(a)** Gains a multiplier while carrying any debuff or negative status · **(b)** Gains a multiplier per debuff or negative status it carries, stacking · **(c)** Gains a multiplier per debuffed card anywhere in its grid, whether or not it is one of them · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0015** `[root]` — **Searing Knife** — skill, A3, from `CARD_CATALOG`. When scored, its column pays 3x this act but is locked (no placement or grabs) for the next 2 turns · **(a)** When this card scores, its column pays triple this act and is locked against placement and grabs for two turns · **(b)** When this card scores, its column pays triple this act and is locked for the rest of the show · **(c)** When this card scores, its column pays triple, and the column to each side is locked for one turn · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0016** `[root]` — **Swashbuckler** — skill, A3, from `balatro`. Paid by what your collection is worth · **(a)** Adds the total sell value of your other cards to the multiplier · **(b)** Adds the total sell value of your other cards to the multiplier, and selling any of them lowers it at once · **(c)** Adds the sell value of your single most valuable other card, doubled · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0017** `[root]` — **The Advance Campaign** — skill, A3, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Cue before a show: spend N Gold tokens to multiply that show's fame payout by 1 + N/10 · **(a)** Cue before a show: spend N gold to multiply that show's payout by 1 + N/10 · **(b)** Cue before a show: spend all your gold to multiply that show's payout by 1 + gold/10, capped at triple · **(c)** Cue before a show: spend N gold to multiply the payout by 1 + N/10, but the next show's goal rises by the same fraction · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0018** `[root]` — **The Kecak Chorus** — skill, A3, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Paid by the low cards on the board · **(a)** When any meld scores, gains a multiplier per rank-1 card on the board · **(b)** When any meld scores, gains a multiplier per card on the board below rank 4 · **(c)** When any meld scores, gains a multiplier per card on the board whose rank is lower than this card's · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0019** `[root]` — **The Wall of Death** — skill, A3, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Both neighbours pay, and both pay for it · **(a)** Both adjacent columns gain a multiplier, and every card in them takes an Exhausted debuff at show end · **(b)** Both adjacent columns gain a multiplier, and one random card in each is Exhausted at show end · **(c)** Both adjacent columns gain a multiplier scaling with how many cards are in them, and all of those cards are Exhausted at show end · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)

### A4 - Higher operators

- **Q0020** `[root]` — **Abstract Card** — skill, A4, from `cryptid`. Raises the score to a power · **(a)** Raises the act's multiplier to a power rather than adding or multiplying, with a chance of being destroyed each show · **(b)** Raises the act's multiplier to a power, with no destruction risk but a very small exponent · **(c)** Raises the multiplier to a power that grows the longer this card survives, with a rising chance of destruction · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0021** `[root]` — **Breaking Infinity** — rule, A4, from solitaire families, mahjong and incremental games. The ceiling itself becomes a thing you remove · **(a)** Rule: score is capped until an effect removes the cap, after which higher operators become available · **(b)** Rule: score is capped until an effect removes the cap; removing it also raises every goal to match · **(c)** Rule: score is capped, and each cap broken permanently unlocks the next operator tier for the profile · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0022** `[root]` — **Critical Deck** — rule, A4, from `cryptid`. A coin flip on the exponent · **(a)** Deck: after each act, a small chance the multiplier is squared, and a smaller chance it is square-rooted · **(b)** Deck: after each act, a small chance the multiplier is squared, with no downside · **(c)** Deck: after each act, the multiplier is raised to a random power between a half and two · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0023** `[root]` — **Googol Play Card** — skill, A4, from `cryptid`. A tiny chance at an absurd number · **(a)** A small chance per act of an enormous multiplier, and nothing otherwise · **(b)** A small chance per act of an enormous multiplier, with a modest one the rest of the time · **(c)** A small chance of an enormous multiplier, and the chance improves each act it fails · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### A5 - Retrigger

- **Q0024** `[root]` — **Double Trigger** — stamp, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. All of this card's effects trigger twice, once per scoring pass · **(a)** All of this card's effects trigger twice · **(b)** All of this card's effects trigger twice, but only on the first line it completes each show · **(c)** This card's effects trigger once more for each line it belongs to, so a card scoring in a row and a column fires three times · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0025** `[root]` — **Echoing Trigger** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. All triggers on the grid repeat once, per card, per scoring pass · **(a)** Every card's triggers on the grid repeat once per scoring pass · **(b)** Every card's triggers in this card's own row and column repeat once · **(c)** Every card's triggers on the grid repeat once, and this card is destroyed at the end of the show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0026** `[root]` — **Exposed** — skill, A5, from `cryptid`. Retriggers the plain ones, kills the faces · **(a)** Retriggers every scoring non-face card twice more, and debuffs every face card · **(b)** Retriggers every scoring non-face card twice more, and face cards simply score normally · **(c)** Retriggers whichever card type is rarer on the board, and debuffs the commoner one · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0027** `[root]` — **Hanging Chad** — skill, A5, from `balatro`. - **Q0028** `[root]` — **Seltzer** — skill, A5, from `balatro`. Retriggers everything, then burns out · **(a)** Retriggers every scoring card for the next ten acts, then is destroyed · **(b)** Retriggers every scoring card for the next three acts, then is destroyed · **(c)** Retriggers every scoring card until it has retriggered a fixed total number of times, then is destroyed · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0029** `[root]` — **The Aerialist** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. - **Q0030** `[root]` — **The Clock Hang** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. May be placed half-off the grid past the last column; while hanging it is double-spotlit (its skill fires twice), but any board-shift effect discards it · **(a)** May be placed half off the grid past the last column; while hanging its skill fires twice, and any board-shift effect discards it · **(b)** May be placed half off the grid past any edge; while hanging its skill fires twice and it cannot be part of any meld · **(c)** May be placed half off the grid; while hanging it fires twice, and it falls off at the end of the act unless a line scored · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q0031** `[root]` — **The Drone Swarm** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. - **Q0032** `[root]` — **The Encore** — stamp, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. If this card's skill triggered this show, there is a seeded 50% chance it triggers once more at show end · **(a)** If this card's skill triggered this show, a 50% chance it triggers once more at show end · **(b)** If this card's skill triggered this show, it triggers once more at show end, guaranteed · **(c)** If this card's skill triggered this show, a 50% chance per trigger that it fires again at show end, capped at three · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0033** `[root]` — **The Fanchant** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. After all scoring resolves, each meld that raised the act's combo pays again at 25% · **(a)** After all scoring resolves, every meld that raised the act's combo pays again at 25% · **(b)** After all scoring resolves, the single highest meld that raised the combo pays again in full · **(c)** After all scoring resolves, every meld that raised the combo pays again at 10% per distinct class involved · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0034** `[root]` — **The Groundhog** — skill, A5, from `CARD_CATALOG`. ### A6 - Score redirect

- **Q0035** `[root]` — **Broken Sync Catalyst** — skill, A6, from `cryptid`. Trades chips for mult · **(a)** Swaps a tenth of the line's flat points into its multiplier · **(b)** Swaps a tenth of the flat points into multiplier, or the reverse, whichever scores higher · **(c)** Swaps a proportion you choose, up to a half, between flat points and multiplier · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0036** `[root]` — **The Accountant** — skill, A6, from `CARD_CATALOG`. At payout, before row x column multiplication, shift up to 20% of the larger axis total onto the smaller, costing 2 combo to invoke · **(a)** At payout, before row times column, shift up to 20% of the larger axis total onto the smaller, at a cost of 2 combo · **(b)** At payout, shift up to 20% of the larger axis total onto the smaller, free of charge · **(c)** At payout, the row and column totals are averaged and both set to that average, at a cost of 2 combo · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0037** `[root]` — **The Credit Line** — skill, A6, from written to fill thin taxonomy classes. Points earned here are paid somewhere else · **(a)** Cue: name another line; for the rest of the act, everything this line scores is paid into that one instead · **(b)** Cue: name another line; half of what this line scores is paid into that one, and the other half scores normally · **(c)** Cue: name another card; everything this line scores is banked onto that card and pays out when it next scores · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0038** `[root]` — **The Running Total** — rule, A6, from reference games. Every line shows what it is worth as you build · **(a)** Rule: each line's accumulating score is visible at its end while you build, not only when it completes · **(b)** Rule: each line's accumulating score is visible, and effects may read it mid-build · **(c)** Rule: each line's total is visible, and a line may be cashed out early at a discount · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### A7 - Scoring gate

- **Q0039** `[root]` — **AP Joker** — skill, A7, from `cryptid`. Saves itself for the boss · **(a)** Scores a large multiplier against bosses only · **(b)** Scores a large multiplier against bosses, and nothing at all otherwise · **(c)** Scores a multiplier that scales with how many bosses you have already beaten this run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)

- **Q0044** `[root]` — **Lot Lice** — rule, A7, from `CARD_CATALOG`. - **Q0045** `[root]` — **Non-scoring bonus card** — skill, A7, from `the random-effects sheet, line 5`. - **Q0046** `[root]` — **Obelisk** — skill, A7, from `balatro`. A whole grid in one suit · **(a)** A new hand: an entire grid of one suit scores enormously · **(b)** A new hand: an entire grid of one suit, or of one suit plus one named exception, scores enormously · **(c)** A new hand: an entire grid using only two suits scores enormously, and one suit scores more again · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q0106** `[root]` — **The Triple** — skill, B1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. ## Family P - Class, group & set synergy


### P1 - Class-count scaling

- **Q0941** `[root]` — **Rehearsal Hall** — rule, P1, from generated. Class counts unlock tiers · **(a)** Rule: two, four and six cards of one class on the board each unlock a stronger bonus for that class · **(b)** Rule: every second card of one class on the board raises that class's bonus by one step, with no cap · **(c)** Rule: two, four and six of one class unlock tiers, and the tiers differ per class · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0942** `[root]` — **The Census Taker** — skill, P1, from reference games. Counts one named thing, anywhere · **(a)** Scores a bonus per card of one named class anywhere on the board, however far away · **(b)** Scores a bonus per card of one named class in its own grid only · **(c)** Scores a bonus per card of the class it has seen least this run, so it chases variety · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q0943** `[root]` — **The Full Troupe** — skill, P1, from generated. Pays for how many of a class are out · **(a)** Scores a bonus for every card of a named class on the board · **(b)** Scores a bonus for every card of a named class on the board, squared rather than summed · **(c)** Scores a bonus for every card of whichever class is most represented on the board · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q0944** `[root]` — **Thin Bill** — skill, P1, from generated. Pays for having almost none · **(a)** Scores a large bonus if exactly one card of its class is on the board · **(b)** Scores a large bonus if it is the only card of its class in the whole deck · **(c)** Scores a bonus scaling inversely with how many of its class are on the board · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### Q8 - Inheritance

- **Q1075** `[root]` — **Sterile Field** — stamp, Q8, from generated. Props arrive clean · **(a)** Props spawned by this card carry no statuses at all, ignoring anything on the spawner · **(b)** Props spawned by this card carry no statuses, and strip statuses from every card they pass · **(c)** Props spawned anywhere on this grid carry no statuses · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1076** `[root]` — **The Absent Master** — rule, Q8, from written to deepen families Q, V, T and O. A prop keeps its spawner even after the spawner is gone · **(a)** Rule: a prop keeps reading its spawner card for its effect, even once that card has left the board · **(b)** Rule: a prop keeps reading its spawner, and if the spawner is destroyed the prop fires one last time at triple strength · **(c)** Rule: a prop that loses its spawner rebinds to the nearest card of the same suit and reads that instead · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1077** `[root]` — **The Burning Volley** — status, Q8, from written to deepen families Q, V, T and O. Fire on the spawner multiplies the volley · **(a)** A card carrying Burning spawns props whose effect is multiplied by the number of fire stacks on it · **(b)** A card carrying Burning spawns one extra prop per fire stack, each at normal strength · **(c)** A card carrying Burning spawns props that apply Burning to every card they cross, spreading the stacks thin · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1078** `[root]` — **The Costume Change** — rule, Q8, from generated. Props carry their spawner's statuses · **(a)** Rule: a prop inherits every status its spawning card carries · **(b)** Rule: a prop inherits one status from its spawner, chosen at random · **(c)** Rule: a prop inherits its spawner's statuses, and applies them to every card it passes · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1079** `[root]` — **The Costume Trunk** — skill, Q8, from written to deepen families Q, V, T and O. Props hand statuses on at the end of the run · **(a)** A prop finishing its path leaves every status it collected on the last card it reached · **(b)** A prop finishing its path leaves the statuses it collected on the cell it stopped in, for the next card placed there · **(c)** A prop finishing its path returns the statuses it collected to its spawner · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1080** `[root]` — **The Handoff** — skill, Q8, from generated. Props pick statuses up as they go · **(a)** A prop takes on any status of the first card it passes, and carries it for the rest of its path · **(b)** A prop takes on the status of every card it passes, accumulating them · **(c)** A prop takes on the status of the first card it passes and leaves that status on the last · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

## Family R - Hazards, bosses & adversity


### R1 - Suit / rank / class debuff

- **Q1081** `[root]` — **Blacklist** — skill, R1, from `cryptid`. Names a rank and kills it · **(a)** Names a rank: any line containing it scores nothing; this card destroys itself once that rank is gone from the deck · **(b)** Names a rank: any line containing it scores nothing, permanently · **(c)** Names a rank each show: lines containing it score nothing, and removing that rank from the deck pays a bounty · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1082** `[root]` — **Firetown's Snub** — rule, R1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Town rule: Flames-suit effects score nothing here, and Burning statuses do not multiply · **(a)** Town rule: one suit chosen per town scores nothing, and its statuses do not multiply · **(b)** Town rule: one suit chosen per town scores nothing, but every other suit scores double · **(c)** Town rule: whichever suit you played most last show scores nothing this show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1083** `[root]` — **Temperance Town** — rule, R1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Town rule: Concessions-class cards are disabled and shop prices are doubled · **(a)** Town rule: Concessions-class cards are disabled and shop prices double · **(b)** Town rule: one class chosen per town is disabled and shop prices double · **(c)** Town rule: one class chosen per town is disabled, and every card of that class in your deck sells for double · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1084** `[root]` — **The Loggione** — rule, R1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. A crowd with expensive taste · **(a)** Town hazard: Common cards score nothing here and Legendary cards score triple · **(b)** Town hazard: cards below a named rarity score nothing here, and the rarity rises each show · **(c)** Town hazard: the rarity you own most of scores nothing here · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1085** `[root]` — **The Pillar** — rule, R1, from `balatro`. Boss: nothing works twice · **(a)** Boss: cards that scored in an earlier act score nothing for the rest of the show · **(b)** Boss: cards that scored in an earlier act score half for the rest of the show · **(c)** Boss: cards that scored in the previous act score nothing this act, but recover the act after · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1086** `[root]` — **The Prepared Town** — rule, R1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. A town that has done its homework · **(a)** Boss: everything is pre-inspected, revealing and disabling every fake, masked and disguised card for the show · **(b)** Boss: everything is pre-inspected and disabled, but each disguised card disabled pays you its rank in gold · **(c)** Boss: one card per act is inspected and disabled, chosen by the boss, in descending order of value · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1087** `[root]` — **Verdant Leaf** — rule, R1, from `balatro`. Everything is off until you sell something · **(a)** Boss: every card is debuffed until you sell one · **(b)** Boss: every card is debuffed until you destroy one of your own · **(c)** Boss: every card is debuffed until you sell one, and the debuff returns each act · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)

The hazards are cards you chose to put down · **(a)** Rule: you place the hazard cards yourself; each one raises the show's difficulty and its reward together · **(b)** Rule: you place hazard cards yourself, and may remove one per act at a cost · **(c)** Rule: you place hazard cards yourself, and adjacent hazards combine into worse ones that pay more · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

Robbers between the turns · **(a)** Between turns, robbers steal the highest-value card on the board · **(b)** Between turns, robbers steal the highest-value card unless it scored that turn · **(c)** Between turns, robbers steal a card, and catching them once ends the thefts for the show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1090** `[root]` — **The Hook** — rule, R2, from `CARD_CATALOG`. ## Family S - Map & run structure


### S1 - Node reveal

- **Q1134** `[root]` — **Fog of war on choices** — rule, S1, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v3 (Biome tour)`. You cannot see far ahead · **(a)** Fog obscures map choices beyond the next step · **(b)** Fog obscures map choices beyond the next two steps · **(c)** Fog obscures choices beyond the next step, and lifts one step further each time you win a show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1135** `[root]` — **The Advance Man** — consumable, S1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Consumable: reveals all fog-of-war nodes within 2 edges of the player's position, including contents · **(a)** Consumable: reveals every fogged map node within two steps, contents included · **(b)** Consumable: reveals every fogged map node on the whole route, but only their types, not their contents · **(c)** Consumable: reveals every fogged node within two steps and lets you swap two of them · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1136** `[root]` — **The Mitt Camp Reading** — consumable, S1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Buys the truth about what is ahead · **(a)** Consumable: pay 1 gold for one true fact about each of the next three map nodes · **(b)** Consumable: pay 1 gold for one true fact about a node of your choice anywhere on the route · **(c)** Consumable: pay 1 gold per node to learn one true fact about it, as many as you can afford · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1137** `[root]` — **The Panorama** — consumable, S1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Shows the shape of the route · **(a)** Consumable: reveals the type of every node on the world map, but not its contents · **(b)** Consumable: reveals the type of every node, and the contents of the next three · **(c)** Consumable: reveals the type of every node for the rest of the run, not just now · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### S2 - Route manipulation

- **Q1138** `[root]` — **Branching escalating-difficulty paths** — rule, S2, from `the braindump, line 721`. Branching paths of rising difficulty · **(a)** Map: branching paths of escalating difficulty, splitting roguelike routes from preset puzzle levels · **(b)** Map: branching paths of escalating difficulty, where clearing one path unlocks a shortcut on another · **(c)** Map: branching paths where each path has its own theme and its own escalating difficulty curve · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1139** `[root]` — **Calendar shows (Inti Raymi)** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A11 South America`. Festivals on a fixed calendar · **(a)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals along the route, every Nth node · **(b)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals, and reaching one early carries the bonus forward · **(c)** Bonus festivals appear at fixed intervals, and you may spend fame to move one closer · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1140** `[root]` — **FTL constellation map** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v4 (FTL, latest)`. A constellation you cannot walk back · **(a)** Map: a constellation of cities with no backtracking; touring builds fame, and hazards and weather occur between them · **(b)** Map: a constellation with no backtracking, and each city visited raises the odds of hazards on the next leg · **(c)** Map: a constellation with no backtracking, and you plan the whole route before the tour begins · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1141** `[root]` — **Map alternative topologies** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v4 (FTL, latest)`. The route has no end, only a shape · **(a)** Map: no final city; the route circles and towns become revisitable once enough time has passed · **(b)** Map: no final city; you must return to where you started, and the return leg is harder · **(c)** Map: the route circles, and each full circuit raises every town's goal · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1142** `[root]` — **Map probability rings** — rule, S2, from `the braindump, line 51`. Better cards toward the middle · **(a)** The map is a square of probability rings, with higher-rank nodes likelier toward the centre · **(b)** The map is a square of probability rings, and travelling inward costs more fame per step · **(c)** The map is a square of probability rings, and the centre is only reachable once per run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1143** `[root]` — **Route-as-song bonus** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A12 Australia & Oceania`. Repeat the route, strengthen the act · **(a)** Repeating a route pattern you have walked before grants a strengthening bonus · **(b)** Repeating a route pattern grants a bonus that grows with how long the repeated stretch is · **(c)** Repeating a route pattern grants a bonus, and repeating it in reverse grants a different one · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1144** `[root]` — **The International Final** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals`. The regions you toured vote · **(a)** Suits favoured by regions you toured generously score extra in the final show · **(b)** Suits favoured by regions you toured generously score extra, and regions you skipped penalise theirs · **(c)** Each region you toured contributes one favoured suit to a final pool, and lines using them score extra · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1145** `[root]` — **The Pitch Lottery** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals`. Bid for first pick · **(a)** Bid gold for first pick of the next three map nodes · **(b)** Bid gold for first pick of the next three nodes; the losing bid is refunded · **(c)** Bid gold for first pick, and the amount bid also determines how much of each node you can see · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1146** `[root]` — **The Route Book** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §8 Map-layer systems`. Plan the route, then stick to it · **(a)** Plan an intended route before the run; deviating from it costs fame · **(b)** Plan an intended route before the run; following it pays a bonus, and deviating costs nothing · **(c)** Plan an intended route; deviating costs fame, but you may re-plan once per biome · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1147** `[root]` — **Triangle map boss phase** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v2 (Triangle)`. Boss phases in the terrain · **(a)** The map places a boss phase every few tiers, drawn as terrain · **(b)** The map places a boss phase every few tiers, and fights happen only at those phases · **(c)** The map places boss phases every few tiers, and you may pay fame to skip one per run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1148** `[root]` — **Triangle map choice** — rule, S2, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v2 (Triangle)`. A triangle you narrow as you climb · **(a)** Choose from the next three options; moving left permanently loses the right lanes, and the middle loses both · **(b)** Choose from the next three options; the lanes you lose become visible so you know what you gave up · **(c)** Choose from the next three; lanes lost reopen once per run at a fame cost · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)

### S3 - Node conversion

- **Q1149** `[root]` — **Location difficulty from card** — rule, S3, from `the braindump, line 48`. The card on the node is the node · **(a)** A map location's suit and rank set its difficulty and its type · **(b)** A map location's rank sets its difficulty and its suit sets its type, and both are visible before travelling · **(c)** A map location's card is the reward you win from it, so choosing the node chooses the prize · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1150** `[root]` — **Monopoly pay-to-unlock squares** — rule, S3, from `the braindump, line 1414`. Pay a card to open the square · **(a)** Some map squares must be unlocked by sacrificing a card before they can be used · **(b)** Some map squares must be unlocked by sacrificing a card of a named class · **(c)** Some map squares must be unlocked with a card, and the card you spend determines the reward · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1151** `[root]` — **Special map squares** — rule, S3, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v1 (Minesweeper)`. Squares that mean something fixed · **(a)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, with higher ranks more likely toward the centre · **(b)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, and one rank is designated the shop rank each run · **(c)** Map squares carry fixed effects by rank, and an effect can change a square's rank to change what it is · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1152** `[root]` — **The Egress (event)** — rule, S3, from `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §9 Events`. A sign that says This Way Out · **(a)** Map event: following a mysterious sign skips the node entirely, with no reward and no risk · **(b)** Map event: following the sign skips the node and pays a small fame bonus for the mystery · **(c)** Map event: following the sign skips the node and reveals what you would have faced · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1153** `[root]` — **The Ghost Train** — rule, S3, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business`. A node you cannot preview · **(a)** Map node: its contents cannot be previewed, and its average reward is better for it · **(b)** Map node: its contents cannot be previewed, and it is always either the best or the worst node available · **(c)** Map node: unpreviewable, with a better average reward, and previewing it at all costs fame · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1154** `[root]` — **The Locked Room** — rule, S3, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §G The Modern Era`. Needs the right keys · **(a)** Map node: requires specific card types as keys to open its reward · **(b)** Map node: requires specific card types as keys, and shows which before you commit to it · **(c)** Map node: requires keys, and each key you lack can be bought with fame instead · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1155** `[root]` — **The Unsanctioned Show** — rule, S3, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §B1 Aerial & Wire`. Perform where you should not · **(a)** Map node: performing at a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and failing loses the run's tips · **(b)** Map node: performing at a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and failing bars you from the region · **(c)** Map node: a forbidden node pays enormous fame, and merely visiting it marks you for a later boss · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

### S4 - Biome / town effects

- **Q1156** `[root]` — **Amusement city (Coney Island)** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A10 The Americas`. A town that is never the same twice · **(a)** Town: its layout and offers differ every visit, and a map event may burn it down so it rebuilds changed · **(b)** Town: its layout and offers differ every visit, and each visit makes the next one stranger · **(c)** Town: its layout and offers are rerolled each visit, and you may pay fame to reroll once more · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1157** `[root]` — **Arena/naumachia town** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A1 The Ancient World`. Only the dangerous acts score · **(a)** Town: only risk-tagged cards score here · **(b)** Town: only risk-tagged cards score, and they score double · **(c)** Town: only risk-tagged cards score, and a flood event fills the bottom rows so nothing may be placed there · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1158** `[root]` — **Bartholomew Fair town** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A2 Medieval & Renaissance Europe`. Every shop at once, and pickpockets · **(a)** Town: every shop type is open at once, but a pickpocket event fires between purchases · **(b)** Town: every shop type is open at once, and prices rise with each purchase · **(c)** Town: every shop type is open at once; a pickpocket fires between purchases, and catching one pays double what it took · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1159** `[root]` — **Cabaret city** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A14 The Caribbean & Central America`. A town that stays up late · **(a)** Town: night-show rules apply, favouring Dancer and Concessions cards · **(b)** Town: night-show rules favour two classes drawn at random for that town · **(c)** Town: night rules favour whichever class you have fewest of, and penalise your most common · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1160** `[root]` — **Exposition City** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business`. One enormous show that takes all comers · **(a)** Town: a single vast goal, once per map, where every suit is welcome and nothing is penalised · **(b)** Town: a single vast goal where every suit is welcome, and the reward scales with how far past it you get · **(c)** Town: a single vast goal where every suit is welcome, but you get one act instead of three · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1161** `[root]` — **Friendly Acrobat town** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A5 China`. A town that likes tumblers · **(a)** Town: Acrobat cards cost less here and an Acrobat pack is guaranteed · **(b)** Town: cards of one class cost less here and a pack of that class is guaranteed, the class differing per town · **(c)** Town: cards matching the class you own most of cost less here · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1162** `[root]` — **Improving town (Chautauqua)** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A10 The Americas`. A town with standards · **(a)** Town: no risk-tagged cards may score, and Concessions cards score double · **(b)** Town: no risk-tagged cards may score, and one class you name at arrival scores double · **(c)** Town: no risk-tagged cards may score, and every clean show raises the town's standing reward · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1163** `[root]` — **Jaded city (Richiardi)** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §C1 Golden-Age Stage Magic`. A town that has seen it all · **(a)** Town: only shock and risk-tagged acts impress here; everything else scores half · **(b)** Town: only shock and risk-tagged acts score at all · **(c)** Town: only acts you have never played in this town before score at full value · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1164** `[root]` — **Outlawed** — type, S4, from `CARD_CATALOG`. In pious/lawful towns this card cannot score at all; everywhere else it scores double · **(a)** In lawful towns this card cannot score at all; everywhere else it scores double · **(b)** In lawful towns this card cannot be placed at all; everywhere else it scores double · **(c)** The first town that outlaws this card makes it score double for the rest of the run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1165** `[root]` — **Pierrot** — skill, S4, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Gains bonus multiplier on odd-numbered shows of the tour; has no effect otherwise · **(a)** Gains a multiplier on odd-numbered shows of the tour and does nothing on even ones · **(b)** Gains a multiplier that grows by one step for each show of the tour it has been played in · **(c)** Gains a multiplier on any show where it is the only card of its class on the grid · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1166** `[root]` — **Resort towns** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business`. An easy stop · **(a)** Town: lower goals, better shops, no boss · **(b)** Town: lower goals and better shops, but no card rewards · **(c)** Town: lower goals and better shops, and the next town's goal rises to compensate · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1167** `[root]` — **The Rappings** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §C2 Séance, Spiritualism & Paranormal`. A town that stays haunted · **(a)** Town: answers come by knocks, and debunking the haunting does not lift it · **(b)** Town: answers come by knocks; debunking it converts the town to a normal one permanently · **(c)** Town: answers come by knocks, and the knocks are honest exactly as often as the town is · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1168** `[root]` — **The Right to Perform** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A14 The Caribbean & Central America`. Comply, or fight for it · **(a)** Town: comply with a ban for no fame, or resist and play a hazard show for double fame and permanent change · **(b)** Town: comply for no fame, or resist for double fame at the risk of losing the town for the run · **(c)** Town: comply, resist, or negotiate, each opening a different show type · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1169** `[root]` — **The Scam Booking** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §F3 Music, Concerts & Festivals`. There is no venue · **(a)** Town: arrive to find no venue; improvise a show where only Commons score, or take the failure · **(b)** Town: arrive to find no venue; improvise where only Commons score, or oversell beyond your deck for a larger prize · **(c)** Town: arrive to find no venue, and choose which restriction the improvised show runs under · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1170** `[root]` — **Town size score requirements** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map v4 (FTL, latest)`. Small towns, big cities · **(a)** Town size sets the score requirement, with small towns cheap and cities dear · **(b)** Town size sets both the requirement and the reward, scaling together · **(c)** Town size sets the requirement, and you choose the size you play when you arrive · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1171** `[root]` — **Tragedy scoring (Circo criollo)** — rule, S4, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A10 The Americas`. Tragedy sells · **(a)** Drama cards score on failed shows, so losing pays something · **(b)** Drama cards score on failed shows, and score more the worse the failure · **(c)** Drama cards score on failed shows, and a failed show with enough of them counts as a win for fame · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)

### S5 - Reward manipulation

- **Q1172** `[root]` — **Add all cards to map** — rule, S5, from `the braindump, line 234`. Every card is somewhere on the map · **(a)** Every card in the game appears somewhere on the map as a collectible node · **(b)** Every card appears on the map, and cards you already own appear as upgrades instead · **(c)** Every card appears on the map, but only those you have unlocked are visible · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1173** `[root]` — **Balanced Sleeve** — rule, S5, from `cryptid`. Everything is equally likely · **(a)** All cards appear in shops with equal probability regardless of rarity, and shops carry extra slots · **(b)** All cards appear with equal probability, with no extra slots · **(c)** All cards appear with equal probability, and rarity instead sets the price · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1174** `[root]` — **Booster node packs** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map (implemented)`. A pack you take whole · **(a)** Booster nodes hand you a five-card pack in full, with no choosing · **(b)** Booster nodes hand you a five-card pack; you keep three and the rest are discarded · **(c)** Booster nodes hand you a five-card pack in full, and one card of your choice leaves the deck · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1175** `[root]` — **Booster Tag** — consumable, S5, from `cryptid`. The next pack is twice the pack · **(a)** Consumable: the next pack holds double the cards and double the choices · **(b)** Consumable: the next pack holds double the cards, but you still keep the same number · **(c)** Consumable: the next three packs each hold one extra card and one extra choice · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1176** `[root]` — **Buttercup** — skill, S5, from `cryptid`. Holds a shop item for later · **(a)** Stores one shop item; selling this card puts the stored item into the next shop · **(b)** Stores one shop item; selling this card gives you the stored item directly · **(c)** Stores up to three shop items, released one per town · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1177** `[root]` — **Disaster card packs** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map content & UX`. A pack of disasters to pick from · **(a)** Disaster packs appear on the map as a choosable option · **(b)** Disaster packs appear on the map; taking one pays fame up front and adds hazards later · **(c)** Disaster packs appear as an option, and refusing one raises the next town's goal · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1178** `[root]` — **Fame gates pack rarity** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §15 Map (implemented)`. Fame buys better odds · **(a)** Accumulated fame raises a run-wide luck value that gates stamp, skill and type rolls in packs · **(b)** Accumulated fame raises pack rarity odds directly, with no separate luck value · **(c)** Accumulated fame raises the luck value, and spending fame at a shop raises it further for one pack · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1179** `[root]` — **Kitty Printer** — skill, S5, from `cryptid`. All rewards become one kind · **(a)** Every skip reward becomes the same named kind · **(b)** Every skip reward becomes a kind you choose at run start · **(c)** Every skip reward becomes the kind you have taken least this run · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1180** `[root]` — **Map high-rarity loose cards** — rule, S5, from `the braindump, line 617`. A single good card lying around · **(a)** The map sometimes offers a single high-rarity card instead of a pack · **(b)** The map sometimes offers a single high-rarity card instead of a pack, at a fame cost · **(c)** The map sometimes offers a single high-rarity card, and refusing it raises the rarity of the next offer · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1181** `[root]` — **Red Card** — skill, S5, from `balatro`. Paid for what you turn down · **(a)** Gains a permanent bonus every time you skip a pack · **(b)** Gains a permanent bonus every time you skip a pack or a map node · **(c)** Gains a permanent bonus per skip, and skipping also reveals what you passed up · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1182** `[root]` — **Redeemed Deck** — rule, S5, from `cryptid`. Upgrades come with their next tier · **(a)** Buying an upgrade also grants its next tier for free · **(b)** Buying an upgrade also grants its next tier, but the tier after costs double · **(c)** Buying an upgrade grants every tier of it at once, and it may never be bought again · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1183** `[root]` — **Showman** — rule, S5, from `balatro`. Duplicates are allowed · **(a)** Cards may appear in shops and packs more than once per run · **(b)** Cards may appear more than once per run, and a second copy of anything costs less · **(c)** Cards may appear more than once, and owning two copies of one card merges them into an upgraded version · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1184** `[root]` — **Special suits via packs only** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §2.4 Suits: 4 + specials`. - **Q1185** `[root]` — **Straw House** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §8 Map-layer systems`. - **Q1188** `[root]` — **The At Liberty pool** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §E The Fairground, Midway & Business`. - **Q1189** `[root]` — **The Boss Trade** — rule, S5, from Slay the Spire, Vault of the Void, Wildfrost, Monster Sanctuary and Griftlands. - **Q1190** `[root]` — **The Carousel** — rule, S5, from Teamfight Tactics and Super Auto Pets. A shared draft you physically move through · **(a)** Rule: at intervals, a rotating ring of cards is offered and you take one by moving to it, with earlier picks going to whoever is furthest behind · **(b)** Rule: at intervals, a ring of cards is offered and picks go in score order, best first · **(c)** Rule: at intervals a ring of cards is offered, each carrying an attached stamp, and you take the pair together · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)
- **Q1191** `[root]` — **The Gathering** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A12 Australia & Oceania`. - **Q1192** `[root]` — **The Gift Exchange** — rule, S5, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A14 The Caribbean & Central America`. - **Q1259** `[root]` — **Glass Stake** — rule, T5, from `cryptid`. - **Q1260** `[root]` — **Horizon Stake** — rule, T5, from `cryptid`. - **Q1261** `[root]` — **Platinum Stake** — rule, T5, from `cryptid`. Four in a row · **(a)** A Connect-4 minigame mode played with placed cards · **(b)** A Connect-4 side objective running alongside normal play, paying a bonus for four of a suit in a line · **(c)** A Connect-4 minigame where the town places against you on the same grid · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)

- **Q1273** `[root]` — **Fishing minigame card** — skill, U1, from `the random-effects sheet, line 7`. Fish a card out of the deck · **(a)** Place this card over the deck to play a fishing minigame for a card from it · **(b)** Place this card over the deck to fish; the harder the fish, the better the card · **(c)** Place this card over the deck to fish, with one attempt per show and a chance of losing the card instead · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1274** `[root]` — **Forbidden-solitaire event mode** — rule, U1, from `DESIGN_DOC.md §21 Events & Encounters`. - **Q1275** `[root]` — **Karuta duel** — rule, U1, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §A6 Japan`. First to spot it · **(a)** A speed-pick minigame where the first correct click takes the reward · **(b)** A speed-pick minigame where each correct click is worth more than the last · **(c)** A speed-pick minigame where a wrong click costs you the round · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (b)
- **Q1276** `[root]` — **Mini Metro mode** — rule, U1, from `the random-effects sheet, line 22`. - **Q1287** `[root]` — **The Faire** — rule, U2, from `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md §G The Modern Era`. ### V1 - Rank comparison override

- **Q1328** `[root]` — **Maximized** — rule, V1, from `cryptid`. Only two ranks exist · **(a)** Every face card counts as the same rank and every number card as the same rank · **(b)** Every card counts as one of two ranks, chosen at run start · **(c)** Every card counts as one of two ranks, and which two changes each show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1329** `[root]` — **Pareidolia** — skill, V1, from `balatro`. Everything is a face card · **(a)** Every card counts as a face card for every effect · **(b)** Every card counts as a face card, and face cards count as no rank at all for runs · **(c)** Every card counts as whichever card type an effect is looking for · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1330** `[root]` — **The Backward-Backward** — skill, V1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Cue: reverse the run direction of one column for the rest of the show, so descending counts as ascending and vice versa · **(a)** Cue: reverse the run direction of one column for the rest of the show, so descending reads as ascending · **(b)** Cue: reverse the run direction of one line for one turn · **(c)** Cue: reverse the run direction of every line in one grid for the rest of the show · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1331** `[root]` — **The Blank** — rank, V1, from `CARD_CATALOG`. This card has no rank or suit: it can stack on anything and anything can stack on it, but it never melds and scores 0 · **(a)** Has no rank and no suit: anything stacks on it and it stacks on anything, but it never melds and scores 0 · **(b)** Has no rank and no suit for stacking, but counts as whatever the line needs when a meld is checked · **(c)** Has no rank and no suit, and any line containing it scores as if it had four cards instead of five · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (a)
- **Q1332** `[root]` — **The Blank Rank** — skill, V1, from written to deepen families Q, V, T and O. A card with no number at all · **(a)** This card has no rank; it may be stacked on anything and anything may be stacked on it, and it counts as no rank for melds · **(b)** This card has no rank for stacking purposes, but takes the rank of whatever it is stacked on for melds · **(c)** This card has no rank until a line containing it scores, at which point it takes whichever rank scores best · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

Copies whatever it can find · **(a)** Rank 0 that also counts as any rank already present in your deck, for stacking and melds; behaves as blank with nothing to copy · **(b)** Rank 0 that counts as any rank present on the board, updating as the board changes · **(c)** Rank 0 that counts as whichever rank would score its line highest · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q1364** `[root]` — **The Wildcard** — skill, V6, from `CARD_CATALOG`. Becomes whatever its row already has · **(a)** On every board change, becomes a copy of a rank and suit already present in its row · **(b)** On every board change, becomes a copy of whichever rank and suit is most common in its row · **(c)** On every board change, becomes whichever rank and suit would score its row highest · **(d)** reject — this effect does not enter the game · *default* (c)

- **Q1370** `[root]` — **The Gap Card** — stamp, V7, from written to deepen families Q, V, T and O. ---

## What this document deliberately does not contain

- **No flowcharts.** Charts redraw questions, and a chart of a thousand independent content
  rulings tells nobody anything. There is no review-canvas stage for this round.
- **No history and no reasoning.** Every effect is stated as trigger, action, number. Where an
  idea arrived with circus history, a Balatro joker name or a paragraph of why it would be fun,
  all of that was stripped. Provenance is kept as a source citation and nothing more.
- **No balance numbers you should trust.** The numbers in the options exist to make the three
  versions distinguishable from each other. Rarity, cost and tuning are a later pass, run against
  `Tools/scoring_sim.py`, not decided here.
- **No implementation.** No pseudocode, no class names, no file paths, no hooks named as
  contracts. An approved effect is a decision that it belongs in the game, not a spec.
- **No engine-capability audit.** The gate that normally precedes a first question round asks
  whether the engine already ships what a design proposes. It does not apply to a content
  selection round — nothing here is being built yet. **Each approved effect still needs that gate
  run against it before it is implemented**, and a cluster of approved effects sharing a mechanism
  should get one design pass between them rather than one each.
- **No family N.** View, camera and UI effects were excluded by your ruling. The taxonomy still
  carries the ten classes so the hole stays visible, and they can be a later round on their own.

## What happens when the round ends

Every answer becomes one row of a single CSV: the effect, the approved variant in full, its slot,
its class, its rarity band, its provenance, and its status. Rejections are recorded as rejections
so the same idea does not get re-mined out of the same documents next time. Free-text answers are
carried verbatim.
